## Grand Prix Charleston 2012

![](https://i.ormos.uk/4c/a7/4ca7f1d6cb6a4232e690a71546fe3d59ddb54f2b8dfb5f09797a377a71730506.jpg)

It's been an incredible weekend for **Magic** here in Charleston. 661 players showed up to compete with their favorite Standard decks. The field was whittled down to 81 players for the second day of competition. When the dust settled only eight players remained. Reid Duke, Peter Kelly, Patrick Cox, Brian Eason, Matt Keene, Gerry Thompson, Morgan Chang, and Jon Bolding were the only Planeswalkers left in the heat of battle.

In the quarterfinals, Jon Bolding dispatched Morgan Chang's four color [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) deck in a tight three games.

The semifinals saw Jon Bolding up against his worst matchup in the top 8, a significantly more aggressive Rakdos deck being piloted by Matt Keene. Bolding managed to curve out perfectly while Keene stumbled a bit with lands that come into play tapped and Jon Bolding was able to escape the semifinals despite being a massive underdog.

Only two were left standing. Brian Eason's powerful Black/Green/White Tokens deck stood no match against Jon Bolding's Rakdos deck that featured [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite) and [Bonfire of the Damned](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonfire+of+the+Damned). Jon Bolding goes home with $3,500, a trophy, and the title of Grand Prix Charleston Champion.

This is Jon Bolding's first major finish and it qualifies him for his first Pro Tour. Be sure to check out Pro Tour Gatecrash to follow Bolding and see if he has what it takes to compete with the best in the world!

## Top 8 - Player Profiles

*by Steve Sadin*

![](https://i.ormos.uk/e6/fb/e6fb12ac4f7ab9bcc8989c42ddf6d8c38ed22369b12e10e2301b2a3751082a31.jpg)

### Pat Cox

**Age:** 28  
**Hometown:** Arlington, VA  
**Occupation:** Systems Engineer  
  

**Previous **Magic** Accomplishments:**  
  
 1 PT top 8, 4 GP top 8s, SCG Invitational Champion

**What deck are you playing, and why did you choose it?**  
  
 Blue/White Flash, because LSV told me to.

**What's your best matchup?**  
  
 The mirror thanks to Talrand

**What's your worst matchup?**  
  
 Reanimator or Junk Tokens

**How big of a factor was [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) in your preparation for this tournament?**  
  
 It was not a major factor.

**If you played your deck again at Grand Prix San Antonio next weekend, what changes would you make (if any)?**  
  
 More Talrands main.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/9d/99/9d99ea2c2bbfb56424c809402b57816f44b1e1b6dfbd91473255a42a9f8f41d8.jpg)

### Matthew Keene

**Age:** 28  
**Hometown:** Charleston, SC  
**Occupation:** Stanley Steamer Associate  
  

**Previous **Magic** Accomplishments:**  
  
 SC States Winner and top 8 finisher last two years. Food article expert.

**What deck are you playing, and why did you choose it?**  
  
 Zombies, I felt like I won by turn four or had enough reach to close out while they tried to recover.

**What's your best matchup?**  
  
 Blue/White Flash

**What's your worst matchup?**  
  
 Blue/White/Red Midrange

**How big of a factor was [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) in your preparation for this tournament?**  
  
 No!!! None at all, if it bleeds we can kill it.

**If you played your deck again at Grand Prix San Antonio next weekend, what changes would you make (if any)?**  
  
 No, not a card.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/28/05/2805be5cdb3a46fdaaecf1128083a18e613be17181f61bb14fe137e028c1db64.jpg)

### Brian Eason

**Age:** 20  
**Hometown:** Atlanta, GA  
**Occupation:** Student  
  

**Previous **Magic** Accomplishments:**  
  
 Money in some GPs, played in some PTs

**What deck are you playing, and why did you choose it?**  
  
 Junk Tokens because Robbie Cordell recommended it and it had the best results on **Magic Online**.

**What's your best matchup?**  
  
 Zombies, Mono Red, Blue/White Flash

**What's your worst matchup?**  
  
 Reanimator w/ [Somberwald Sage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Somberwald+Sage)

**How big of a factor was [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) in your preparation for this tournament?**  
  
 Somewhat, I'm playing Primal Hunter because it's very good vs. control and [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk).

**If you played your deck again at Grand Prix San Antonio next weekend, what changes would you make (if any)?**  
  
 Cut the Conclave [Blessing](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blessing) for a [Garruk Relentless](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk+Relentless). Cut the Sigarda for a third [Ultimate Price](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ultimate+Price) in the sideboard.

## Quarterfinals Round-Up

*by Jacob Van Lunen*

### Pat Cox (Blue/White Flash) vs. Brian Eason (Black/Green/White Tokens)

### Game 1

Brian started to accelerate with [Arbor Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arbor+Elf) while Pat set up his hand with [Augur of Bolas](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Augur+of+Bolas).

Brian began making a board presence with [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls), establishing a dominant board presence. An attack for two and Flashback put Pat Cox in quite the predicament. [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim) came down and things were looking good for the GP top 8 rookie.

Pat Cox had [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) when Brian attacked, but Pat tapping out meant that Brian had a chance to resolve [Collective Blessing](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Collective+Blessing). The next turn he was swinging for twenty damage and Pat could barely hang on, another [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) came down and Pat was in chump blocking mode. One more attack was all it took for Brian to get the first game.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/74/d5/74d585b9466315a57f85c3dfb13ec1e6f65262ae639196308f6638ef573d1b82.jpg)

*Pat Cox*

### Game 2

Game two won't be quite as easy for Brian Eason once Pat Cox has access to [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict) and [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere). Pat is hoping his sideboard will be strong enough to overcome one of his more difficult matchups.

Again, Brian ramped into [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) with [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim) and once again the game looked like it was swinging heavily into Brian's favor very early.

An all out attack from Brian on turn three met a [Snapcaster Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snapcaster+Mage) from Pat Cox that traded with [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim).

Brian continued charging into the red zone, but didn't want to overcommit to the board, knowing that Pat had sideboarded in his [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict)s. Pat declined to cast his [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel), intending on wiping the board before he spent any resources.

A [Sphinx's Revelation](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx%27s+Revelation) for two from Pat made it seem like he was a huge favorite to win the game.

An all out attack from Brian dropped Pat to nine, but he was casually leaning back in his seat, hoping that Brian would commit more resources before he cast [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict).

Pat pulled further ahead with another [Sphinx's Revelation](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx%27s+Revelation), this time for three.

[Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere) came down for Pat and he targeted a Spirit, Brian decided to cast [Ultimate Price](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ultimate+Price) targeting his own spirit, but Pat had [Dissipate](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dissipate) to ensure that all the spirits would be going to detention.

Brian reestablished his board presence, with [Borderland Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Borderland+Ranger) and another [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls). [Intangible Virtue](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Intangible+Virtue) came down and once again Brian was looking to be in the driver seat.

Pat had a lot of digging power and he ran through his deck looking for another [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere) or a [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict). Despite seeing a ton of cards, Pat was unable to find one of these important spells.

Brian looked like he was running out of gas, though, and Pat still had plenty of cards in hand. Pat continued digging and eventually found a [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict).

Brian had lethal in the air with his Spirits and Pat was forced to cast Surpeme Verdict despite Brian still having two copies of [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) in his graveyard and [Intangible Virtue](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Intangible+Virtue) in play. One copy of lingering souls met [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate), but the second copy resolved.

Pat found another copy of [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict) on the top of his library and began to completely dominate the game.

Brian was out of cards and things didn't look good against Pat's full grip, but Brian wasn't out of this just yet, he cast [Garruk, Primal Hunter](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk%2C+Primal+Hunter) and made a token.

Pat wasn't going to let Garruk get in his way, though and he quickly killed Garruk with Restoration angel and a Spirit.

With [Dissipate](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dissipate) and [Rewind](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rewind) in hand, and a dominant board position, Pat quickly closed the game out with a [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel), some spirits and [Runechanter's Pike](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runechanter%27s+Pike) while Brian floundered, looking for some glimmer of hope.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/20/dd/20dd33a548bce2fe56837137a6b9a663190f883ec8615a2d678f1caf542fd637.jpg)

*Brian Eason*

### Game 3

Brian had [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim) on the first turn and [Arbor Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arbor+Elf) on the second. He went for [Garruk Relentless](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk+Relentless) on the third turn and Pat was forced to let it resolve.

Brian tried to give Garruk a friend in [Sorin, Lord of Innistrad](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sorin%2C+Lord+of+Innistrad), but Pat had [Dissipate](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dissipate). Garruk continued powering out tokens and Pat struggled to find an answer to the powerful Planeswalker.

[Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) proved good enough to deal with [Garruk Relentless](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk+Relentless), but Pat was facing down a massive army of wolves.

[Sorin, Lord of Innistrad](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sorin%2C+Lord+of+Innistrad) came down for Brian on his fifth turn. He made an Emblem and was able to attack for more than enough damage to kill his opponent.

Brian Eason 2 - 1 Pat Cox

### Gerry Thompson (Blue/White Flash) vs. Matt Keene (Rakdos Aggro)

### Game 1

Matt Keene got off to an aggressive start with [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) into Knight of infamy, [Bump in the Night](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bump+in+the+Night) and another [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) had Gerry on the ropes very quickly. Another Bump in the night dropped Gerry down to five before he had his fourth land in play. Gerry tried to stabilize, but Matt's draw was far too aggressive and Gerry found himself dead before he had a chance to get fancy.

Matt Keene 1 - 0 Gerry Thompson

### Game 2

Matt went down to five cards but still had an aggressive start. [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) seemed to be particularly problematic for Gerry. Gerry's draw wasn't well equipped to deal with the Protection from White two drop and his life total dwindled.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f2/7f/f27f4fff0794e63d6ed4a437f7aebfb3763f9648f29b44d6a2e41d6b43c2473a.jpg)

*Gerry Thompson*

Gerry couldn't get his footing while being beaten down by the Knight. [Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger) only made things worse. Once again, Gerry was burned to a crisp in the blink of an eye.

Matt Keene 2-0 Gerry Thompson

## Quarterfinals - Reid Duke (Bant Control) vs. Peter Kelly (White Green Humans)

*by Steve Sadin*

Reigning **Magic Online** Champion Reid Duke is playing in his fourth Grand Prix Top 8 this weekend. He's hoping that he'll be able to earn his second Grand Prix title with a Bant splash [Nephalia Drownyard](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nephalia+Drownyard) control deck that (on paper) seems like it would be particularly well suited against his Quarterfinal opponent's White Green Humans deck.

However, first time Grand Prix Top 8 competitor Peter Kelly's deck is quite a bit better against slow control decks than most aggressive White Green decks are. He can hold opponents off of key spells with Judge's Familiar and [Thalia, Guardian of Thraben](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thalia%2C+Guardian+of+Thraben) – and he can keep the pressure on even after a [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict) thanks to cards like [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor), and [Doomed Traveler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doomed+Traveler).

### Game 1

Both players mulliganed down to five, and while the only early play that Duke could muster was a turn two [Ground Seal](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ground+Seal) -- Kelly had a ton of early pressure with double [Champion of the Parish](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Champion+of+the+Parish), [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor), and a [Knight of Glory](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Glory).

[Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict) wiped that away, but a Judge's Familiar plus the automatically recursed [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor) allowed Kelly to keep the pressure on.

[Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) put Duke back to 14 – but a [Silverblade Paladin](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silverblade+Paladin) gave Kelly's Judge's Familiar double strike and set up an attack for 6 that knocked Duke down to 8.

A second [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk), and then a third [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) helped Reid a bit – but with no way to stop the double striking 3/1 Judge's Familiar, it was only a matter of time before he died to the little bird that could.

Peter Kelly 1 – Reid Duke 0

### Game 2

Avacyn's Pilgrim, [Doomed Traveler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doomed+Traveler), [Thalia, Guardian of Thraben](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thalia%2C+Guardian+of+Thraben), and a [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor) allowed Kelly to get in for a few points of damage early – but Duke prevented things from getting out of hand by ramping up his mana with [Farseek](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Farseek), and wiping the board with a fourth turn [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict).

Kelly made a spirit token when his [Doomed Traveler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doomed+Traveler) died, and quickly enchanted it with a [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor). However, the potent combination of [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk), plus [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) allowed Duke to put himself back up to 20 life while generating an army of 11 power worth of attackers.

Kelly had no way to keep up, and pretty soon the players were off to a deciding third game.

Peter Kelly 1 – Reid Duke 1

### Game 3

Kelly got off to a very threatening start in the third game with Avacyn's Pilgrim, [Fencing Ace](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fencing+Ace), and a [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor) -- while Duke spent his early turns trying to set things up for himself by casting [Farseek](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Farseek), [Elixir of Immortality](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Elixir+of+Immortality), and [Think Twice](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Think+Twice).

Once he was down to 6, and staring down what would be lethal damage, Duke used a [Terminus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminus) to wipe away his opponent's board.

Undeterred, Bolding cast an end of turn [Selesnya Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Selesnya+Charm) to generate a token, enchanted it with [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor), and attacked knocking Duke down to 2. [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) brought Duke back up to 7, but a [Silverblade Paladin](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silverblade+Paladin) plus a second [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor) were enough for Kelly to take the match.

Peter Kelly 2 – Reid Duke 1

## Quarterfinals - Morgan Chang (4 Color Reanimator) vs. Jon Bolding (Black Red Zombies)

*by Steve Sadin*

Long time grinder Morgan Chang earned the first Grand Prix Top 8 of his career at Vancouver earlier this season – but he fell just short of earning an invitation there, taking a bittersweet exit in the Quarterfinals. A few short months later, and Chang finds himself with a chance to do one better here in Charleston.

But in order for Chang to earn a coveted invitation to Pro Tour Gatecrash, he's going to need his 4 Color Reanimator deck to outlast first time Grand Prix Top 8 competitor Jon Bolding's lightning fast Black Red Zombie deck.

### Game 1

A pair of [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul)s, and a [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) gave Bolding a lot of pressure early – but Chang did what he could to stabilize the board with a [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel), and a [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls).

However, that would prove to be too little too late, as the protection from white [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy), plus direct damage from Geralf's Messenger, [Brimstone Volley](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brimstone+Volley), and [Searing Spear](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Searing+Spear) allowed Bolding to get off to a quick lead in the match.

Jon Bolding 1 – Morgan Chang 0

![](https://i.ormos.uk/08/0a/080af06c5e5c5d4bdeea50eb4a7794c2db1995a0faa6e2f29c4a4dea677f75ed.jpg)

*Jon Bolding*

### Game 2

Bolding opened the second game with a [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy), a [Searing Spear](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Searing+Spear) that took out a [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman), and a [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat). Once he got to 4 mana, Chang played a [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) – but that quickly died to a [Victim of Night](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Victim+of+Night), and the ensuing attack knocked him down to 9. Another [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) followed, and Chang was left with no choice but to cast a relatively ineffective [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict).

In response to the [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict), Bolding sacrificed both of his Knights to the [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat), boosting his flier up to 6 power, and allowing it to survive through his opponent's sweeper.

Chang followed that up with a [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman), and he was left hoping that his opponent didn't have a way to finish him off.

The next attack left Chang at 3, and while Bolding didn't have a burn spell to finish things off – he did have another creature in the form of [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler).

[Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere) took out the [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat), but Chang still couldn't afford to breathe easy as a freshly cast [Hellrider](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellrider) set up an attack that put Chang on 1, and left him without a board.

[Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) put Chang back up to 6, but things were still looking very dicey for the Grand Prix Vancouver Top 8 alum.

Bolding played a second [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) before attacking with his [Hellrider](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellrider) and [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler). Chang eagerly traded [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) for his opponent's [Hellrider](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellrider), but he still fell down to 2 from the attack.

Another [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk), brought Chang back to 7 – but the 5/3 beast wouldn't be long for the world as a [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) set up an attack with a 3/2 [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) that Chang decided to block with his [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk). Bolding brought the [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) back, and passed his turn to Chang who had a board full of lands, and a pair of 3/3 beast tokens.

[Abrupt Decay](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Abrupt+Decay) killed off the [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy), and once again left Bolding without any good attacks – however, a Geralf's Messenger once again swung things slightly in Bolding's favor.

However, Bolding's lead would quickly disappear.

[Tracker](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tracker)'s Instinct put an [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) into Chang's graveyard and a [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) into his hand. The [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) brought back a [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk). After spending most of the game just struggling to survive Chang, now found himself at a relatively healthy 10 life and in a spot where he could quickly pull away should he be able to live through another couple of turns.

[Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) allowed Bolding to get in a clean swipe for 3 with a [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler), but at this point things were rapidly slipping away for him. A [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat) gave Bolding a bit of hope -- but between a pair of [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman)s, and [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) Chang soon found himself well outside of burn range, and in a dominating position on board.

Bolding stuck around for a few more turns, but once the game had gotten to that point it was only a matter of time before he fell to Chang's overwhelming forces.

Jon Bolding 1 – Morgan Chang 1

![](https://i.ormos.uk/4a/c8/4ac83bad6b5c45484deca1d4b156138abc7d22d4c6f0250ec7eb9673c22c9fb4.jpg)

*Morgan Chang*

### Game 3

Bolding had an ideal opening in the third game with a [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler), and a [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy), while Chang didn't have a play before his third turn [Centaur Healer](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Centaur+Healer) (which promptly died to a [Searing Spear](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Searing+Spear)). By the time that Chang had the mana to cast a [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel), Bolding had found a second [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) – setting up an attack that took Chang to 8.

Rather than cast the contextually ineffective [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) that was sitting in his hand, Chang instead used a [Forbidden Alchemy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forbidden+Alchemy) to dig for an answer. His choice paid off, as he found a [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict) to clear the board.

However, that would only offer Chang a brief reprieve as Bolding was able to untap and cast a hasty [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite) that clocked Chang down to a mere 3 life.

Desperate for an answer to his opponent's dragon, Chang cast a mainphase [Forbidden Alchemy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forbidden+Alchemy). This time, however, no answer was forthcoming.

Chang lingered over the board for a moment, before extended his hand – wishing his opponent good luck in the Top 4.

Jon Bolding 2 – Morgan Chang 1

## Semifinals - Peter Kelly (Green/White Humans) vs. Brian Eason (Black/Green/White Tokens)

*by Jacob Van Lunen*

Peter Kelly and Brian Eason are both making their Grand Prix top 8 debut. Peter Kelly has completely steamrolled this tournament with an unorthodox Green/White Humans deck of his own design. His opponent, Brian Eason, is playing Black/Green/White Tokens, a deck that's been putting up very impressive **Magic Online** numbers over the past few days.

### Game 1

Both players kept their opening hands and Peter had the first play of the game with [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim). Brian made an [Arbor Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arbor+Elf).

Peter sent his Pilgrim into the red zone and Brian took the damage. Another [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim) and [Judge's Familiar](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Judge%27s+Familiar) came down to give Peter the early board advantage.

Brian had a [Borderland Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Borderland+Ranger) to take control, he found a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) and used it to cast [Arbor Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arbor+Elf).

Peter decided to up the ante with [Sublime Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sublime+Archangel). [Judge's Familiar](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Judge%27s+Familiar) came in for five damage. Brian needed to answer the angel or come up with a reasonable plan for racing if he was going to have a chance.

[Judge's Familiar](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Judge%27s+Familiar) came in again and Brian cast [Midnight Haunting](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Midnight+Haunting). One spirit jumped in front of the [Judge's Familiar](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Judge%27s+Familiar) and Peter dumped the remainder of his hand, [Fencing Ace](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fencing+Ace) and [Mayor of Avabruck](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mayor+of+Avabruck), onto the table.

Brian couldn't find an answer to the [Sublime Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sublime+Archangel) and it quickly became apparent that Peter was in the driver seat.

Peter made an interesting play, sending with a good portion of his team instead of maximizing the value of his [Sublime Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sublime+Archangel). This gave Brian the opportunity to get back into the game with another copy of midnight haunting.

Suddenly, Brian looked to be pulling into the lead. Garruk Primal Hunter only elicited a shrug from Peter and it became apparent that Peter would need a lot of help to win the game.

Peter found a [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor) (exactly what he needed!) on the top of his deck and bashed with an enchanted [Fencing Ace](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fencing+Ace). Brian threw his team in front of the double striking trampler, but Peter's last card was [Selesnya Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Selesnya+Charm) and Brian died to the massive attack.

Peter Kelly 1 - Brian Eason 0

![](https://i.ormos.uk/46/30/4630853bc59e8b42a0fd3926015d9e48e2b121e5ddaf1823cbd21b1e13c1cd2e.jpg)

*Peter Kelly*

### Game 2

Brian opened with [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim) and Peter had [Judge's Familiar](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Judge%27s+Familiar). [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim) helped power out a [Centaur Healer](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Centaur+Healer) from Brian, but Peter had a pair of [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor)s for his [Judge's Familiar](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Judge%27s+Familiar) and Brian found himself facing down a five power flyer on turn two.

[Judge's Familiar](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Judge%27s+Familiar) traded with a Spirit and Brian fell to 11. Peter's [Fiend Hunter](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fiend+Hunter) took care of Brian's Pilgrim, but Brian had [Intangible Virtue](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Intangible+Virtue) and used the Flashback on Lingering souls.

Peter decided to cast [Thalia, Guardian of Thraben](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thalia%2C+Guardian+of+Thraben) and [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor) up his First Striker. Brian was content to send his flyers into the red zone and advance his board position a bit with [Borderland Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Borderland+Ranger).

Brian's Spirit tokens were quickly whittling away at Peter's life total and it quickly became apparent that he wouldn't be able to race the flying squadron of Spirits.

Peter Kelly 1 - Brian Eason 1

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f5/75/f575727f1dfc0c024b4679acc807d2d82b5271c5a321f3889b05c262a20ec6d6.jpg)

*Brian Eason*

### Game 3

Peter powered himself ahead with [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim) on the play when Brian didn't have a one mana accelerator. [Thalia, Guardian of Thraben](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Thalia%252C%2BGuardian%2Bof%2BThraben), then [Mayor of Avabruck](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mayor+of+Avabruck) came down and Peter looked to be stealing this game before Brian could get any footing.

Brian tried to buy himself time with [Centaur Healer](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Centaur+Healer), but Peter continued beating in and cast [Fencing Ace](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fencing+Ace) to put himself even further ahead. [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) from Brian wasn't going to be enough to stop Peter's team. [Centaur Healer](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Centaur+Healer) traded with [Fencing Ace](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fencing+Ace), but [Thalia, Guardian of Thraben](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thalia%2C+Guardian+of+Thraben), [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim), and [Mayor of Avabruck](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mayor+of+Avabruck) were still doing fine work.

[Garruk Relentless](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk+Relentless) from Brian tried to deal with [Thalia, Guardian of Thraben](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thalia%2C+Guardian+of+Thraben), but Peter chose to cast [Faith's Shield](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faith%27s+Shield) to continue pulling ahead.

Peter didn't see [Garruk Relentless](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk+Relentless) as a threat and sent his team at Brian again. Brian traded a Spirit for [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim) and got to untap with 6 life, a planeswalker, and a Spirit token. Now the game looked to be swinging into Brian's favor.

Brian found another copy of [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) and the game shifted to his advantage. [Intrepid Hero](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Intrepid+Hero) came down and made sure that Peter wouldn't be able to end the game out of nowhere with [Sublime Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sublime+Archangel) or [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor).

Brian had pulled far enough ahead that he was able to start getting in for damage. With his spirits. The unanswered planeswalker on Brian's side of the table was proving to be very difficult for Peter. Peter found a [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor), but with an army of Spirits and Intrepid hero on Brian's side, it didn't seem all that impressive.

Brian's board started to get comically big as he continued activating Garruk and even had a [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) with [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel). A massive attack from Brian sent Peter into the tank. Peter was all out of options and he was forced to chump block with his entire team in order to survive. One more attack was all it took for Brian to win the game.

Peter Kelly 1 - 2 Brian Eason

## Semifinals - Matt Keene (Black Red Zombies) vs. Jon Bolding (Rakdos Zombies)

*by Steve Sadin*

Matt Keene, and Jon Bolding were both ecstatic after they won their Quarterfinal matches and earned themselves invitations to Pro Tour Gatecrash. But now that their primary goals for the tournament had been accomplished, Keene and Bolding got to set their sights a bit higher...

### Game 1

Keene, who was on the play, missed a beat because he had a pair of [Rakdos Guildgate](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Guildgate)s, and a [Dragonskull Summit](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dragonskull+Summit) as his only lands.

"I need you to have lands like that to have a chance," said Bolding, referencing just how difficult it would be for his deck to break serve when Keene was on the play.

By the time Keene had played a [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul) and a pair of Grave Crawlers, Bolding had already deployed a [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul), and a [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) of his own. However, Bolding's mana base was also taking its toll on him, as he wound up paying 4 life for a pair of [Blood Crypt](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Crypt)s that allowed him to play his spells on time.

The two players played Geralf's Messengers – and continued attacking each other with minimal resistance.

But once he was on 8 life, Bolding realized that he needed to hold back all three of his creatures if he wanted to live through his opponent's next (sure to be bloody) attack. Keene's ensuing attack with everything knocked Bolding down to 5, while Keene himself fell to 10 when Bolding's Geralf's Messenger recursed itself back into play.

Post-combat, Keene cast a [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy), and brought back his two [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler)s – putting himself into a position where he would easily be able to attack for lethal damage on his next turn.

However, he would never get that turn as Bolding had a [Brimstone Volley](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brimstone+Volley), and a [Hellrider](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellrider) to give himself exactly enough damage to take the first game.

Jon Bolding 1 – Matt Keene 0

![](https://i.ormos.uk/95/a1/95a1728cb1571a9d3e30c5e7caa30777e3069199dafcce22cb80678c8d6b51ab.jpg)

*Matt Keene*

### Game 2

Keene got off to a great start in the second game with a [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler), a [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul), and a Geralf's Messenger.

And while Bolding did what he could to keep up with a pair of [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler)s, and a [Pillar of Flame](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pillar+of+Flame) to exile the Geralf's Messenger – a [Bump in the Night](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bump+in+the+Night), a [Searing Spear](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Searing+Spear), and a [Brimstone Volley](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brimstone+Volley) was more than enough burn to allow Keene to even the match at one game apiece.

Jon Bolding 1 – Matt Keene 1

![](https://i.ormos.uk/d7/e3/d7e3f30e18c3ee9d586d4812add85c3f7061720e1fa0e90305151ac2af78cd2b.jpg)

*Jon Bolding*

### Game 3

Keene mulliganed down to 5, and while he was somewhat relieved by the fact that Bolding's first play was a [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy), he still found himself backpedaling in a hurry.

After opening with a first turn [Bump in the Night](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bump+in+the+Night) Keene drew a [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) of his own on his second turn, and was forced to take 2 damage from his [Blood Crypt](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Crypt) to cast it.

When Bolding had a [Searing Spear](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Searing+Spear) to clear out Keene's [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy), and a [Pillar of Flame](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pillar+of+Flame) to exile the Geralf's Messenger that followed it, pretty soon Keene was down to 12.

Not only that, but Bolding was able to keep his offenses flowing with a [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler), and a [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul).

Keene managed to find a [Pillar of Flame](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pillar+of+Flame), and a [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul) of his own to slow things down a bit, and give himself at least a chance to outrace his opponent with [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat).

Despite his best efforts, Keene was ultimately unable to pull off the miraculous comeback, as Bolding had the firepower that he needed to take the match.

Jon Bolding 2 – Matt Keene 1

## Finals - Brian Eason (Black/Green/White Tokens) vs. Jon Bolding (Rakdos)

*by Jacob Van Lunen*

Out of a field of 661 players, only two remain. Jon Bolding followed up his undefeated day 1 record with a top 8 berth, his innovative high end Rakdos deck has proven itself to be the real deal. Brian Eason and his Black/Green/White Tokens deck have been unbeatable on the second day of competition, let's see if Brian can continue his incredible run.

### Game 1

Both players spent the first few turns playing lands. A [Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger) from Jon was the first play of the game, but Brian had a [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) to stay at parity.

[Hellrider](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellrider) came down for Jon and he sent both his creatures at Brian. Brian elected to chump block one of the 3/3s, but still found himself taking five damage and falling to 13.

Jon sent [Hellrider](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellrider) and [Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger) again and Brian attempted to triple block the [Hellrider](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellrider). A [Searing Spear](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Searing+Spear) from Jon meant that [Hellrider](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellrider) would live to attack again. Another [Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger) joined the team and Brian got a one-way ticket to frown town.

Brian had a [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) for the next two consecutive turns, but he was forced to stay on the defensive and Jon continued getting in for damage.

Jon found a [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) and started maximizing his [Hellrider](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellrider) triggers by only attacking with [Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger)s without +1/+1 counters and his [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler). Brian needed to find a way back into the game, but Jon didn't seem to be cutting him any slack. [Garruk, Primal Hunter](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk%2C+Primal+Hunter) drew five cards, but Brian couldn't find an answer and Jon sent his team in for lethal damage on the following turn.

Who says [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) has made red decks obsolete? Jon Bolding certainly disagrees.

Jon Bolding 1 - 0 Brian Eason

![](https://i.ormos.uk/0b/d9/0bd9557c48c95942579044f4396f66a0c2e05a2f61c790c62d6fb95830268ec8.jpg)

*Jon Bolding*

### Game 2

Jon cast [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul) as the first play of the game. Brian showed off some of his sideboard strength with [Centaur Healer](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Centaur+Healer), but Jon had [Victim of Night](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Victim+of+Night) and continued getting in with [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul).

[Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger) came down for Jon and Brian looked to be on the defensive again.

Jon thought for a bit when Brian passed the turn with four lands open, he didn't want to get caught attacking into a [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel). After an intellectual catharsis, he decided to send with just his [Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger). Brian did have the Angel and he was able to block the Messenger and set off its undying trigger.

Another [Centaur Healer](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Centaur+Healer) looked to be swinging the game into Brian's Favor, but Jon tapped out for [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite) and Brian was forced to take five damage.

[Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite) came in again and Brian elected to block and activate his [Vault of the Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+of+the+Archangel) to trade his Angel for the Dragon and 3 life. Jon had a [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) to add to his board presence.

[Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger) traded with [Centaur Healer](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Centaur+Healer) and [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) started to get in. Brian had [Midnight Haunting](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Midnight+Haunting) at the end of Jon's turn and [Garruk Relentless](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk+Relentless) came down and fought with [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul) to put Brian way ahead.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ed/0f/ed0f32df7d49c6346d1ba43398b69f7ae354cfe103e7a87f664dc94a5c0a6010.jpg)

*Brian Eason*

Brian's [Garruk Relentless](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk+Relentless) did some heavy lifting and [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) plus flashback meant that Jon would need a [Bonfire of the Damned](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonfire+of+the+Damned) or [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite).

As it turned out, Jon already had [Bonfire of the Damned](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonfire+of+the+Damned) and he cast it (not miracled) for three, killed all the tokens, burned Brian, and was able to attack and kill [Garruk Relentless](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk+Relentless) with his [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler).

[Hellrider](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellrider) was next for Jon and Brian was back on his heels.

[Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) off the top was a pretty nice one for Brian, but Jon's [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat) wasn't going to be blocked by the ground beast any time soon.

Brian had a seemingly endless stream of tokens to throw in front of the 4/1 flyer. [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) and flashback were coupled with Intangible virtue and Brian started getting some damage in.

All Brian's efforts were for naught; Jon drew [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite) and dropped it onto the table. Even the [Selesnya Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Selesnya+Charm) in Brian's hand wasn't enough to stop Jon's Rakdos army.

Jon Bolding 2 - 0 Brian Eason

Jon Bolding is your Grand Prix Charleston Champion!

## The Top 5 Card of Grand Prix Charleston 2012

*by Steve Sadin*

![Garruk Relentless](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Garruk+Relentless)

5) [Garruk Relentless](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk+Relentless)

The relatively underplayed Planeswalker [Garruk Relentless](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk+Relentless) managed to overperform for eventual finalist Brian Eason's token deck all weekend long. [Garruk Relentless](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk+Relentless) was largely responsible for Brian's wins in both his Quarterfinals, and his Semifinals matches -- completely dominating the board, killing pesky creatures left and right, and flooding the table with wolves.

![Cavern of Souls](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Cavern+of+Souls)

4) [Cavern of Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cavern+of+Souls)

[Counterspell](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterspell)s have been increasingly popular in *Return to Ravnica* Standard, and [Cavern of Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cavern+of+Souls) is one of the best ways to fight them. It's gotten to the point where even the majority of dedicated control copies are running 2-3 [Cavern of Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cavern+of+Souls), and just about every aggressive deck is packing a full four copies to help them power their way past countermagic.

![Supreme Verdict](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Supreme+Verdict)

3) [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict)

[Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict) catapulted from being a reasonably good sideboard card in Blue White decks to an absolute all star as Black Red Zombies, White Green Humans, and a host of other creature decks packing 4 copies of [Cavern of Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cavern+of+Souls) have risen to prominence.

The format is no longer defined by control mirrors, and consequently the latest iteration of [Wrath of God](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wrath+of+God) will be an important tool going forward.

![Thalia, Guardian of Thraben](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Thalia%2C+Guardian+of+Thraben)

2) [Thalia, Guardian of Thraben](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thalia%2C+Guardian+of+Thraben)

Blue White Flash was considered by many to be "the deck to beat" coming into this event. Many players turned to [Cavern of Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cavern+of+Souls) as their anti-control card -- but Cavern does nothing to slow down [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict).

And while Black Red Zombie decks have burn, haste, and undying creatures to allow themselves to take games after getting their boards swept -- aggressive white decks just don't have that flexibility...

That's where [Thalia, Guardian of Thraben](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thalia%2C+Guardian+of+Thraben) comes in.

[Thalia, Guardian of Thraben](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thalia%2C+Guardian+of+Thraben) caused some serious headaches for control players this weekend, allowing White Green Aggro players to put their Blue White Flash, and Bant Control opponents off of key spells just long enough to win their matches with minimal (Sphere of) resistance.

![Thundermaw Hellkite](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Thundermaw+Hellkite)

1) [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite)

Jon Bolding's [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite)s earned their keep in the finals against Brian Eason's token deck. The ***Magic** 2013* Mythic Rare is putting itself in a position to supplant even [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) as the premier five-mana spell of the Standard format.

It kills tokens, Planeswalkers, and people!

Grand Prix Bochum was also a standard event, and occurred this weekend, too. Why not [check out the top 5 cards](https://web.archive.org/web/20160725082533/http://magic.wizards.com/en/events/coverage/j%C5%AFza-repeats-bochum) from GB Bochum, and see how the Continental European angle on this weekend's Standard event was unique?
